## 3 The Twin-Prefix Framework

## 3.1 Problem Formulation

Fi<sub>x a po</sub>li<sub>cy</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t<sub>, a</sub>t <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>superv</sub>i<sub>s</sub>i<sub>on po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>, sen</sub>d<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e ver</sub>ifi<sub>er</sub> a plan prefix of length L and accepts or rejects on its verdict. A call reviewing L items costs

$$
C ( L ) = F + L \cdot i \qquad \mathrm { v e r i f i e r ~ t o k e n s } ,\tag{1}
$$

with fixed overhead F (instruction, <sub>g</sub>oal, executed context) <sub>an</sub>d <sub>per-</sub>it<sub>em</sub> <sub>cos</sub>t $i ,$ i<sub>.e.</sub> $F / L + i$ <sub>per</sub> <sub>rev</sub>i<sub>ewe</sub>d <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ep,</sub> f<sub>a</sub>lli<sub>ng</sub> <sub>mono</sub>t<sub>on</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> i<sub>n</sub> $L \colon$ <sub>cos</sub>t <sub>a</sub>l<sub>one argues</sub> f<sub>or</sub> l<sub>ong w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ows.</sub> The counterweight is verdict quality. Writing catch(L) for the probability that a bad prefix of length L is rejected (the safet<sub>y</sub> term) and $\mathrm { F R } ( L )$ f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e pro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>a ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d clean prefix is rejected (false rejection: forfeited usefulness), <sub>we score a w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ow</sub> b<sub>y</sub> it<sub>s</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orme</sub>d<sub>ness</sub>

$$
J ( L ) = \operatorname { c a t c h } ( L ) - \operatorname { F R } ( L ) .\tag{2}

This is Youden’s index (Youden 1950; Powers 2011), the <sub>mon</sub>it<sub>or</sub>’<sub>s</sub> b<sub>a</sub>d<sub>-</sub>f<sub>rom-c</sub>l<sub>ean</sub> di<sub>scr</sub>i<sub>m</sub>i<sub>na</sub>ti<sub>on:</sub> $J { = } 0$ i<sub>s c</sub>h<sub>ance</sub> l<sub>eve</sub>l<sub>,</sub> $J { = } 1$ perfect separation. J makes rejectivity, the propensity to reject regardless of label, visible (a rejecteverything verifier reaches catch = 1 yet $J \ = \ 0 ) ;$ it i<sub>s</sub> <sub>p</sub>revalence-free and wei<sub>g</sub>hs both error t<sub>yp</sub>es equall<sub>y</sub> (unequal costs: Section 3.4).

Why this construction. Instrument vs. deployment semantics. The twins are judged by two independent, stateless verifier <sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>s</sub> <sub>per</sub> <sub>pa</sub>i<sub>r</sub> <sub>an</sub>d l<sub>eng</sub>th<sub>,</sub> <sub>one</sub> <sub>per</sub> t<sub>w</sub>i<sub>n,</sub> <sub>never</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th i<sub>n</sub> <sub>one</sub> <sub>promp</sub>t<sub>,</sub> <sub>ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>hi<sub>ng</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>l<sub>oymen</sub>t<sub>,</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>mon</sub>it<sub>or</sub> <sub>never</sub> sees a labeled <sub>p</sub>air (trans<sub>p</sub>ort: A<sub>pp</sub>endix AC); each call returns accept/reject with one of eight registered reason codes (taxonom<sub>y</sub>: A<sub>pp</sub>endix E).

the best reject-if- $\cdot s { \ge } \theta$ o<sub>p</sub>eratin<sub>g</sub> <sub>p</sub>oint at that len<sub>g</sub>th (the maximum includes reject-nothing, so $J ^ { * } ( L ) \geq 0 )$ <sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e pa</sub>i<sub>r</sub> <sub>separa</sub>t<sub>es ran</sub>ki<sub>ng</sub> l<sub>oss</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>res</sub>h<sub>o</sub>ld d<sub>r</sub>ift<sub>;</sub> S<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> 4<sub>.</sub>3 <sub>runs</sub> thi<sub>s</sub> t<sub>es</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

The per-item price i in Eq. 1 is unit-independent, so <sub>a</sub>ll <sub>a</sub> l<sub>onger un</sub>it b<sub>uys</sub> i<sub>s amor</sub>ti<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>over</sub>h<sub>ea</sub>d<sub>: ex</sub>t<sub>en</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> $L  L ^ { \prime } > L$ saves $\dot { F ( 1 / L - 1 / L ^ { \prime } ) }$ t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>ens</sub> <sub>per</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ep.</sub> Wh<sub>a</sub>t it risks is discrimination. With the shadow value λ pricing that risk (verifier tokens <sub>p</sub>er action, <sub>p</sub>er unit of $J ) ,$ th<sub>e ex</sub>t<sub>ens</sub>i<sub>on</sub> <sub>pays</sub> if th<sub>e</sub> <sub>sav</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>covers</sub> th<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orme</sub>d<sub>ness</sub> l<sub>oss:</sub>

## 4 Experiments

W<sub>e</sub> d<sub>es</sub>i<sub>gn</sub> <sub>exper</sub>i<sub>men</sub>t<sub>s</sub> t<sub>o</sub> <sub>answer</sub> f<sub>our</sub> <sub>ques</sub>ti<sub>ons:</sub>

• RQ1: Does a lon<sub>g</sub>er reviewed unit bu<sub>y</sub> a fallible monitor more discrimination, or only more rejection?

• RQ2: Where and wh<sub>y</sub> do lon<sub>g</sub> windows fail?

• RQ3: Is the sha<sub>p</sub>e robust across jud<sub>g</sub>es<sub>, p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>ts<sub>,</sub> and <sub>pro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l<sub>s</sub>?

• RQ4: Does an<sub>y</sub> realizable <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> beat a calibrated fixed <sub>s</sub>h<sub>or</sub>t <sub>un</sub>it?

## 4.1 Experimental Setup

Verifiers. The primary verifier is Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct (bf16) (Grattafiori et al. 2024); the executor/draft model is Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct (Int4) (Yan<sub>g</sub> et al. 2024); the same 14B, se<sub>p</sub>aratel<sub>y</sub> instanced (Int4 in retail as frozen; bf16 in A<sub>p</sub>- pWorld), is the size-contrast verifier; Qwen2.5-72B-Instruct (bf16) is the cross-famil<sub>y</sub> verifier. The <sub>p</sub>anel unconfounds scale from linea<sub>g</sub>e (14B vs. 72B: ca<sub>p</sub>acit<sub>y</sub> within one famil<sub>y</sub>; 70B vs. 72B: famil<sub>y</sub> at matched ca<sub>p</sub>acit<sub>y</sub>). The o<sub>p</sub>en-wei<sub>g</sub>ht trio re-judges the frozen corpora byte-identically at temperature 0 through one gateway; three closed-weight judges f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>ree o</sub>th<sub>er prov</sub>id<sub>ers rep</sub>li<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>e</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th d<sub>oma</sub>i<sub>ns un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e score-mo</sub>d<sub>e promp</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub>i<sub>r own</sub> t<sub>ranspor</sub>t<sub>s,</sub> di<sub>sc</sub>l<sub>ose</sub>d rather than matched (Table 1; A<sub>pp</sub>endix I). Cost constants are fitted from batched forward runs: F≈655 fixed tokens per call, i≈135 per step $( R ^ { 2 } { = } 0 . 9 8 ;$ A<sub>pp</sub>endix R).

Domains. We evaluate a τ<sup>2</sup>-style retail tool agent (Barres et al. 2025) and A<sub>pp</sub>World (Trivedi et al. 2024) multi-a<sub>pp</sub> API programs, projected into one action-frame abstraction, jud<sub>g</sub>ed under a domain-neutral <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>t (A<sub>pp</sub>endix AC); the <sub>pa</sub>i<sub>r</sub> <sub>spans</sub> <sub>s</sub>h<sub>or</sub>t<sub>-</sub>h<sub>or</sub>i<sub>zon</sub> <sub>sc</sub>h<sub>ema-</sub>b<sub>oun</sub>d di<sub>a</sub>l<sub>ogues</sub> <sub>an</sub>d l<sub>ong-</sub> <sup>horizon</sup> <sup>m</sup>u<sup>lti-a</sup>pp p<sup>ro</sup>g<sup>rams</sup>, <sup>so</sup> <sup>a</sup> <sup>sha</sup>p<sup>e</sup> <sup>re</sup>p<sup>eatin</sup>g <sup>across</sup> b<sub>o</sub>th i<sub>s no</sub> f<sub>orma</sub>t <sub>ar</sub>tif<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

## 4.4 RQ3: The Shape Is Robust

Clean prefixes contain no injected content, so the rejectivity half of every claim does not rest on injection validity (Section 3.2); on the catch half, every injected write changes database state relative to its gold twin (200/200 retail; 188/188 evaluable AppWorld), task success is defined <sub>on</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e, an</sub>d thi<sub>s env</sub>i<sub>ronmen</sub>t <sub>census, no</sub>t th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>p</sub>anel, carries label validit<sub>y</sub> (dis<sub>p</sub>ute set em<sub>p</sub>t<sub>y</sub>, ar<sub>g</sub>maxes unchan<sub>g</sub>ed under a two-model blind panel; Appendix Q).

Closed judges and budgets reproduce the decay. Three closed-weight judges (DeepSeek-V4-Pro, Claude Opus 4.8, GPT-5.6 Sol; no Qwen/Llama self-preference) replicate the shape: every closed column peaks at L=1 (Table 1), the retai <sub>ran</sub>ki<sub>ng</sub> d<sub>ecays</sub> f<sub>or a</sub>ll th<sub>ree</sub> i<sub>n score mo</sub>d<sub>e, a</sub> hi<sub>g</sub>h<sub>er s</sub>t<sub>ar</sub>t d<sub>oes</sub> not help (Claude Opus 4.8: 0.9995 single-action ranking), <sub>an</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> A<sub>pp</sub>W<sub>or</sub>ld <sub>none o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> th<sub>ree score con</sub>t<sub>ras</sub>t<sub>s exc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>d<sub>es</sub> 0, matchin<sub>g</sub> the 70B and 72B cells (A<sub>pp</sub>endix I). Across the wider batter<sub>y</sub> (<sub>p</sub>ara<sub>p</sub>hrase, thinkin<sub>g</sub> on and of, a com<sub>p</sub>ositi<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>corpus, s</sub>h<sub>or</sub>t<sub>-on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng,</sub> d<sub>e</sub>f<sub>er on</sub> d<sub>ec</sub>id<sub>e</sub>d it<sub>ems,</sub> closed judges in score mode) all fifteen 95% intervals on the short-window advanta<sub>g</sub>e exclude zero (A<sub>pp</sub>endices I, O, S, W, X), and anchor/task rewei<sub>g</sub>htin<sub>g</sub> kee<sub>p</sub>s short ahead 12/12 scheme×cell (ar<sub>g</sub>max 11/12; A<sub>pp</sub>endix R). ## 4.5 RQ4: A Short Unit Is Rarely Beaten

## 5 Limitations

Injected errors. Injections are single, non-adaptive writes b<sub>oun</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e ca</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h h<sub>a</sub>lf<sub>; a un</sub>it<sub>-aware p</sub>l<sub>anner</sub> i<sub>s ou</sub>t<sub>s</sub>id<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>es</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>reg</sub>i<sub>me;</sub> <sub>na</sub>t<sub>ura</sub>l<sub>-error</sub> <sub>pro</sub>b<sub>es</sub> <sub>agree</sub> <sub>on</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>ca</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h <sub>r</sub>i<sub>se,</sub> not the J peak (Appendices $\bar { \mathbf { B } } , \mathrm { L } , \mathrm { Q } , \bar { \mathbf { X } } )$

Verifier coverage and scope. Zero-shot panel, two tool-<sub>agen</sub>t d<sub>oma</sub>i<sub>ns,</sub> $L { \leq } 8 ,$ <sub>no secon</sub>d<sub>-</sub>f<sub>am</sub>il<sub>y process-rewar</sub>d <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>;</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>argmax</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>per-</sub>d<sub>ep</sub>l<sub>oymen</sub>t<sub>;</sub> <sub>c</sub>l<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ms</sub> <sub>scope</sub> t<sub>o</sub> <sub>per-ca</sub>ll <sub>prox</sub>i<sub>es.</sub>

Statistical. Retail gold-clean prefixes cap at n=31, so small FR <sub>gaps</sub> <sub>a</sub>r<sub>e</sub> <sub>u</sub>nd<sub>e</sub>r<sub>powe</sub>r<sub>e</sub>d <sub>a</sub>nd th<sub>e</sub> <sub>pea</sub>k <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ays</sub> <sub>p</sub>r<sub>o</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l<sub>-</sub> sensitive; costs are verifier tokens alone (A<sub>pp</sub>endices AF, Z).

## C Per-Step Protocol Control (Full Curves)

The main paper’s protocol control re-judges the frozen retail cor<sub>p</sub>us with <sub>p</sub>er-ste<sub>p</sub> verdicts (the verifier labels each of the L window steps independentl<sub>y</sub> in one call) aggregated b<sub>y</sub> any-reject.

Denominator policy. The 14B returns a few unusable verdicts per length (usable bad n 187–200, clean 30–31 of 200/31). The main <sub>p</sub>a<sub>p</sub>er’s Table 1 kee<sub>p</sub>s <sub>p</sub>er-len<sub>g</sub>th denominators; analyses that need the complete item×L matrix (the <sub>score-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>pass,</sub> th<sub>e orac</sub>l<sub>e</sub>/<sub>po</sub>li<sub>cy ana</sub>l<sub>yses, an</sub>d thi<sub>s con-</sub> trol’s holistic reference) dro<sub>p</sub> incom<sub>p</sub>lete items $\mathrm { ( } n { = } 1 7 4$ b<sub>a</sub>d / 29 clean), whence small ofsets against Table 1 (e.g. 14B $J ( 2 ) { = } 0 . 4 3 1$ vs 0.422; clean FR(1) 0.069 vs 0.065); the perstep re-judge itself has full coverage (200 bad / 31 clean per len<sub>g</sub>th, both verifiers). Table A5 <sub>g</sub>ives the full clean falsereject curves under both protocols. The rise with L survives the protocol change on both verifiers tested; the short-L orderin<sub>g</sub> fli<sub>p</sub>s between verifiers (70B <sub>p</sub>er-ste<sub>p</sub> FR is lower at $L { = } 1$ <sub>, exac</sub>t M<sub>c</sub>N<sub>e</sub>m<sub>a</sub>r $p = 0 . 0 0 8 \mathrm { : }$ ; the 14B’s is hi<sub>g</sub>her), so <sub>ne</sub>ith<sub>er pro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l d<sub>om</sub>i<sub>na</sub>t<sub>es,</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t <sub>no pro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l t<sub>es</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ma</sub>k<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>ong w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ow sa</sub>f<sub>e.</sub> T<sub>wo rea</sub>di<sub>ngs o</sub>f th<sub>e per-s</sub>t<sub>ep ver</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t <sub>vec</sub>t<sub>or</sub> exist: any-reject (reject the window if any step is rejected) and the closure reading (accept the longest all-accepted leading run; the window passes if that run covers all L steps). By construction the two share the same clean false-reject curve (a clean window is falsel rejected if an of its steps is rejected under either reading), and the identity was verified exactl<sub>y</sub> (0 mismatches on both verifiers).

## J Oracle Bound, Router, and Verdict Pooling

## M Observation Deprivation: Intervention and Structural Audit

Design (pre-registered before any judgment ran). Len<sub>g</sub>thenin<sub>g</sub> the review window simultaneousl<sub>y</sub> (a) adds ste<sub>p</sub>s to review and (b) de-executes ste<sub>p</sub>s that, in the gold trajectory, had produced observations. The bound gener-<sub>a</sub>li<sub>zes across a</sub>ll <sub>s</sub>i<sub>x ce</sub>ll<sub>s:</sub> th<sub>e ep</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>em</sub>i<sub>c-c</sub>l<sub>ass s</sub>h<sub>are o</sub>f <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ean</sub> rejections rises with $L$ (retail 70B $0 . 1 5  0 . 7 2$ <sup>b</sup><sub>y</sub> $L { = } 5 )$ <sub>w</sub>hil<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>a</sub>d<sub>-s</sub>id<sub>e s</sub>h<sub>are o</sub>f <sub>ca</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>es</sub> i<sub>s compara</sub>bl<sub>y</sub> l<sub>arge a</sub>t $L { = } 8 ( 0 . 8 8 5 / 0 . 8 4 7 / 0 . 5 7 5 \colon$ retail 14B, A<sub>pp</sub>World 14B, 72B), so full conversion widens the long-L informedness envelope (retail 70B $L { = } 8 \colon [ - 0 . 3 4 1 , 0 . 4 8 6 ] \tilde { \rangle }$ while leaving short-L envelopes tight ([0.331, 0.410] at L=1): even before the mea-<sub>suremen</sub>t b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>ow, a</sub> th<sub>ree-way reg</sub>i<sub>me cou</sub>ld <sub>no</sub>t b<sub>e assume</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> f<sub>avor</sub> l<sub>ong w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ows.</sub>

Retail: long windows buy abstention, not discrimination. On retail (70B, all 1,215 windows judged, zero transport failures), the abstention channel takes on the lar<sub>g</sub>e lon<sub>g</sub>- <sub>w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ow s</sub>h<sub>are</sub> th<sub>e reco</sub>d<sub>e</sub> b<sub>oun</sub>d <sub>an</sub>ti<sub>c</sub>i <sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d<sub>:</sub> th<sub>e c</sub>l<sub>ean-s</sub>id<sub>e</sub> d<sub>e</sub>f<sub>er ra</sub>t<sub>e c</sub>li<sub>m</sub>b<sub>s</sub> $0 . 0 0 0 / 0 . 0 6 5 / 0 . 3 2 3 \dot { / } 0 . 4 5 2 / 0 . 6 1 3$ across $L { = } 1 / 2 / 3 / 5 / 8$ (bad side 0.000–0.415), decided coverage on clean windows collapses to 0.387 at $L { = } 8 .$ <sub>, an</sub>d 93 of the 95 $L { = } 8$ d<sub>e</sub>f<sub>erra</sub>l<sub>s carry</sub> th<sub>e ep</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>em</sub>i<sub>c co</sub>d<sub>e</sub> NEED\_OBSERVATION (18 of the 19 clean-side deferrals and 75 of the 76 bad-side ones; the main paper’s Section 4.4 <sub>quo</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ean-s</sub>id<sub>e</sub> <sub>sp</sub>lit<sub>,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>l<sub>oymen</sub>t<sub>-</sub>f<sub>ac</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>one,</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> $\mathtt { m 3 \_ a 1 \_ j u d g m e n t s \_ d e f e r \_ 7 0 b .  j s o n l } \mathtt { d o m 1 } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { \_ { n } } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { \_ { n } } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { \_ { n } } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { \_ { n } } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { \_ { n } } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { d o m } \mathtt { \_ { n } }$ <sub>.</sub> O<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e su</sub>b<sub>-</sub> set the verifier itself chose to decide, selective J still peaks at $L { = } 2 \ ( 0 . 6 2 3 )$ and collapses to 0.177 at $\scriptstyle L = 8 ;$ th<sub>e pa</sub>i<sub>re</sub>d <sub>con-</sub> trast selective-J(8)−J(2) is −0.446, CI [−0.656, −0.208], excludin<sub>g</sub> zero (a blinded derived contrast: the anal<sub>y</sub>sis, including this contrast, was coded before any judgment returned, but the specific choice of the L=2 anchor and difference CI is not itself <sub>p</sub>re-re<sub>g</sub>istered). The residual decided rejections also remain over-eager on clean windows (selective FR 0.161 at L=1 vs 0.750 at L=8).

Neither deployment mapping rescues long windows. Treating deferrals as rejections gives $J 0 . 5 7 9  0 . 0 5 2$ f<sub>rom</sub> $L { = } 1 \operatorname { t o } 8$ ; treating them as acceptances gives 0.579 → 0.285; both peak at short L. ## Q Label-Validity Audit: Environment Census and Model Panel

Layer 1: exhaustive environment-semantics census (zero model calls). For every design=main bad item, the injected write and its gold twin write are each applied to the <sub>same rep</sub>l<sub>aye</sub>d <sub>anc</sub>h<sub>or s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e an</sub>d th<sub>e resu</sub>lti<sub>ng</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a</sub>b<sub>ase s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> difed. Retail: 200/200 bad writes environment-accepted and immediately state-divergent from the gold twin (stateequivalent dispute set: empty); 24 writes are no-ops relative to the <sub>p</sub>re-state (the<sub>y</sub> still diver<sub>g</sub>e from <sub>g</sub>old, disclosed se<sub>p</sub>- aratel<sub>y</sub>); wherever a <sub>g</sub>old sufix exists the two arms are additi<sub>ona</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> <sub>ro</sub>ll<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>orwar</sub>d<sub>,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> di<sub>vergence</sub> <sub>pers</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>s</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> terminal state (106/106 evaluable); every clean twin is the gold anchor write itself, environment-accepted (31/31). AppWorld: 188/188 evaluable bad items accepted and immediately divergent, persisting to the terminal state (188/188); 2 items are not evaluable because the gold anchor write itself returns HTTP 422 in re-re<sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub> (a <sub>g</sub>old-intrinsic issue; divergent under both readings); 16 items’ gold sufixes raise in-sufix errors (disclosed; terminal dif still com<sub>p</sub>uted); noops: 2; clean 200/200. A determinism control replays three <sub>anc</sub>h<sub>ors</sub> th<sub>ree</sub> ti<sub>mes eac</sub>h<sub>:</sub> b<sub>y</sub>t<sub>e-</sub>id<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es.</sub> T<sub>as</sub>k <sub>success</sub> i<sub>n</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k<sub>s</sub> i<sub>s</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>on exac</sub>tl<sub>y</sub> thi<sub>s</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a</sub>b<sub>ase s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e,</sub> so “bad ̸= gold-equivalent” is the environments’ own semantics rather than an annotator’s judgment.

Layer 2: two-model blind panel. 150 bad–clean pairs sampled by strata (severity 47/66/37 S-A/B/C; 131 maindesign plus 19 position-design units with a guaranteed minimum <sub>p</sub>er non-em<sub>p</sub>t<sub>y p</sub>osition cell), <sub>p</sub>resented blinded (X/Y <sub>ran</sub>d<sub>om or</sub>d<sub>er, no</sub> ki<sub>n</sub>d<sub>-revea</sub>li<sub>ng</sub> fi<sub>e</sub>ld<sub>s;</sub> l<sub>ea</sub>k<sub>age scan as-</sub> serted) to two annotator models outside the judged pipeline: GPT<sub>-</sub>5<sub>.</sub>6 <sub>an</sub>d Cl<sub>au</sub>d<sub>e</sub> O<sub>pus</sub> 4<sub>.</sub>8<sub>, eac</sub>h <sub>answer</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>our</sub> re<sub>g</sub>istered questions (bad substantivel<sub>y</sub> violates the task? <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ean va</sub>lid? <sub>equ</sub>i<sub>va</sub>l<sub>en</sub>t l<sub>ega</sub>l <sub>p</sub>l<sub>an</sub>? <sub>reason co</sub>d<sub>e cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t with rationale?) with <sub>y</sub>es/no/uncertain. A<sub>g</sub>reement: bad-side <sub>v</sub>i<sub>o</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>es</sub> $\kappa { = } . 7 0 5$ (raw .86), clean-side valid $\kappa { = } . 2 9 8$ (raw .63); per-question κ $. 6 4 / . 6 2 / . 4 7 / . 7 1$ . The low clean-side κ is abstention, not contradiction: of the 131 census-covered b<sub>a</sub>d <sub>un</sub>it<sub>s,</sub> GPT<sub>-</sub>5<sub>.</sub>6 <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub>l<sub>y con</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>a</sub>b<sub>e</sub>l <sub>once an</sub>d Opus 4.8 twice (agreeing 77 and 89 times, the rest “uncertain”); on the clean side the counts are 1 and 0 contradictions (68 and 124 agreements; GPT-5.6 marks 81 clean units uncertain). Cross-checked a<sub>g</sub>ainst the la<sub>y</sub>er-1 environment criterion, panel–census agreement is .588/.679 (bad side) and .453/.827 (clean side); no unit has both annotators asserti<sub>ng</sub> “b<sub>o</sub>th <sub>p</sub>l<sub>ans</sub> l<sub>ega</sub>l<sub>.</sub>” A<sub>s pre-</sub>d<sub>ec</sub>l<sub>are</sub>d<sub>:</sub> th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>pane</sub>l i<sub>s</sub> <sub>no</sub>t <sub>a</sub> h<sub>uman au</sub>dit <sub>an</sub>d <sub>pane</sub>l <sub>agreemen</sub>t i<sub>s no</sub>t <sub>groun</sub>d t<sub>ru</sub>th<sub>;</sub> th<sub>e opera</sub>ti<sub>ve va</sub>lidit<sub>y ev</sub>id<sub>ence</sub> i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e env</sub>i<sub>ronmen</sub>t <sub>census,</sub> the panel a corroborating, fully disclosed layer. (19 sampled <sub>pos</sub>iti<sub>on-</sub>d<sub>es</sub>i<sub>gn un</sub>it<sub>s</sub> h<sub>ave no census row, s</sub>i<sub>nce</sub> th<sub>e census</sub> covers design=main, and are excluded from cross-check denominators.)

Layer 3: exclusion stability. The registered dispute set (la<sub>y</sub>er-1 state-equivalent ∪ both-annotator concordant veto) is empty, so the registered exclusion rerun changes noth-<sup>i</sup>n<sub>g</sub> $( \Delta { = } 0$ in ever<sub>y</sub> cell). Two harsher variants: a conservative single-veto set (any one annotator vetoes; retail 10 bad +1 clean, AppWorld 1 bad) moves no rate by more than 0.024; a double-afirmed-only set (drop every label not afirmatively endorsed by both annotators, i.e. counting abstentions as exclusions; retail 23/15, AppWorld $4 0 / 4 \bar { 6 } )$ moves rates by up to 0.131 yet leaves every argmax unchanged and sharpens the retail peak $( J ( 2 ) \ \mathrm { 0 . 6 0 1 } ^ { - }  \ 0 . 7 2 2 .$ , J(8) $0 . 0 3 5  - 0 . 0 2 8 )$ <sub>.</sub> R<sub>eason co</sub>d<sub>es rema</sub>i<sub>n ver</sub>ifi<sub>er-em</sub>itt<sub>e</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>no</sub> h<sub>uman or mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>re-co</sub>di<sub>ng.</sub>

F≈655 and i≈135 <sub>are</sub> fitt<sub>e</sub>d<sub>, no</sub>t <sub>pos</sub>it<sub>e</sub>d<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e w</sub>idth <sub>exper</sub>i<sub>men</sub>t <sub>runs</sub> th<sub>e same</sub> <sub>agen</sub>t t<sub>as</sub>k<sub>s</sub> li<sub>ve a</sub>t $N \in \{ 2 , 3 , 4 , 6 , \bar { 8 } \}$ <sub>p</sub>l<sub>anne</sub>d <sub>ac</sub>ti<sub>ons un-</sub> der two su<sub>p</sub>ervision arms (one call <sub>p</sub>er action, and one call for the whole batch) and re<sub>g</sub>resses the batched arm’s per-call verifier tokens on N; the fit is linear with intercept 655.1 and slope 135.3 tokens per reviewed item (cellmean $R ^ { 2 } = 0 . 9 8 4 \dot { 4 }$ , code/scripts/plot\_width.py on $\exp \_ w \mathrm { i d t h . j s o n 1 ) }$ . F is therefore the fixed mass one forward call carries whatever L is, the instruction, the output schema, the goal and the tool surface; i is the marginal cost <sub>o</sub>f <sub>one</sub> <sub>more</sub> <sub>rev</sub>i<sub>ewe</sub>d <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ep.</sub>

## Y One Frozen Four-Exemplar Protocol and Its Decomposition

Protocol. Result: a diferent operating point, not a better verifier. The exemplars shrink the collapse: paired against the <sub>same-con</sub>fi<sub>g con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l<sub>,</sub> $[ J ( 2 ) - J ( 8 ) ]$ falls by 0.406 (cluster CI [−0.588, −0.186] for the signed diference; identical against the frozen leg). But J alone flatters this: at L=8 the control sits in near-blanket rejection (catch 0.969, FR 0.923, J 0.045) and the exemplars cut FR to 0.385 while cutting catch to 0.786 (Table A28). The gain is bought by rejecting less, <sub>w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>a</sub>l<sub>so ca</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>es</sub> f<sub>ewer rea</sub>l <sub>errors; un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> th<sub>e paper</sub>’<sub>s co-</sub> <sub>pr</sub>i<sub>mary</sub> <sub>sa</sub>f<sub>e</sub>t<sub>y</sub> di<sub>sc</sub>i<sub>p</sub>li<sub>ne</sub> thi<sub>s</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>a</sub> dif<sub>eren</sub>t <sub>opera</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> <sub>po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>,</sub> <sub>no</sub>t <sub>a</sub> d<sub>om</sub>i<sub>na</sub>ti<sub>ng ver</sub>ifi<sub>er.</sub>

Model and estimators. The per-call series answers what <sub>one</sub> <sub>ver</sub>ifi<sub>er</sub> <sub>ca</sub>ll di<sub>scr</sub>i<sub>m</sub>i<sub>na</sub>t<sub>es;</sub> <sub>a</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>l<sub>oymen</sub>t <sub>exper</sub>i<sub>ences</sub> episodes: an N-step task supervised at unit L issues at most $\scriptstyle { m = \lceil N / L \rceil }$ <sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>s,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>a</sub> <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ean</sub> <sub>ep</sub>i<sub>so</sub>d<sub>e</sub> <sub>surv</sub>i<sub>ves</sub> <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> if <sub>ev-</sub> ery call accepts. The protocol is halt-on-reject throughout, <sub>an</sub>d <sub>every rea</sub>d<sub>ou</sub>t b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>ow</sub> i<sub>s c</sub>h<sub>arge</sub>d <sub>un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>seman</sub>ti<sub>cs.</sub> W<sub>r</sub>iti<sub>ng</sub> $\bar { S } _ { k }$ for the probability that the first k calls all accept $( S _ { 0 } { = } 1 )$ , call k+1 is issued exactly when the first k accept, <sub>so</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>expec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>num</sub>b<sub>er</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>s</sub> i<sub>ssue</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>o</sub>k<sub>en</sub> <sub>cos</sub>t <sub>per</sub> <sub>comp</sub>l<sub>e</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>ep</sub>i<sub>so</sub>d<sub>e are</sub>

$$

## AA A Partial-Commit Policy on Per-Step Verdicts

Design. The visible horizon need not equal the commitment horizon. The per-step protocol (Appendix C) already separates them: one call reads all L steps and labels each. The <sub>p</sub>artial-commit <sub>p</sub>olic<sub>y</sub> PC(L) executes onl<sub>y</sub> the window’s l<sub>ea</sub>di<sub>ng accep</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>run</sub> $( \ell _ { \mathrm { s e m } } ,$ th<sub>e reg</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ere</sub>d l<sub>ea</sub>di<sub>ng-</sub>ACCEPT closure) and replans at the first rejection; the registered contrast B(L) is the whole-window binar<sub>y</sub> on the same verdicts (commit all L if ever<sub>y</sub> step accepts). Because the injected <sub>wr</sub>it<sub>e occup</sub>i<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>s</sub>l<sub>o</sub>t<sub>,</sub> PC <sub>re</sub>l<sub>eases</sub> it <sub>exac</sub>tl<sub>y w</sub>h<sub>en</sub> B d<sub>oes:</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>erm</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l b<sub>a</sub>d<sub>-wr</sub>it<sub>e re</sub>l<sub>ease ra</sub>t<sub>e</sub> i<sub>s</sub> id<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>l b<sub>y con-</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on, so</sub> PC <sub>can on</sub>l<sub>y move c</sub>l<sub>ean</sub> th<sub>roug</sub>h<sub>pu</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d <sub>cos</sub>t<sub>.</sub> Retail onl<sub>y</sub> (no A<sub>pp</sub>World <sub>p</sub>er-ste<sub>p</sub> cor<sub>p</sub>us); <sub>p</sub>er-ste<sub>p</sub> FR levels difer from the holistic <sub>p</sub>rotocol’s (A<sub>pp</sub>endix C).

Reading: long-window rejections concentrate at the window tail. At L=8 the leading accepted run averages 4.19 of 8 steps: the whole-window verdict discards clean work its own per-step labels accept. PC therefore commits 0.524 of clean steps where B commits 0.065 (throughput gain CI [0.345, 0.559], excluding zero) and cuts tokens per committed clean action from 3,362 to 414 [352, 504] at the same 0.015 terminal release rate. The 14B reproduces the direction (PC(8) 373 tokens per committed action; terminal release 0.000; B(8) commits nothing). Exploratory: on the interior-position rows (L=8, bad write at position $4 ; n { = } 6 0 )$ PC releases the interior bad write at 0.0 in both cells, the <sub>per-s</sub>t<sub>ep pro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ay</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>er</sub>i<sub>or-sens</sub>iti<sub>ve w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> th<sub>e</sub> h<sub>o</sub>li<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> <sub>pro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l i<sub>s no</sub>t<sub>,</sub> th<sub>oug</sub>h thi<sub>s</sub> i<sub>s a s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e pos</sub>iti<sub>on</sub> d<sub>es</sub>i<sub>gn.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>accep</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d h<sub>ea</sub>d i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>groun</sub>d<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> <sub>par</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> <sub>w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ow,</sub> <sub>con-</sub> sistent with the de<sub>p</sub>rivation mechanism (A<sub>pp</sub>endix M); PC is a test-set readout of an untuned policy, and it refines the <sub>s</sub>h<sub>or</sub>t<sub>-un</sub>it <sub>rec</sub>i<sub>pe</sub> <sub>ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> <sub>rescu</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>o</sub>l<sub>e-w</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ow</sub> l<sub>ong</sub> <sub>ver</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>s.</sub>
